Let's focus on a strong optimizer with corrected gradient and global diversity. The final program can be moderately concise but robust. Need ensure no lambdas, top-level helpers. Use global `height_sequence_1` if available.

Key components:
- `fast_self_conv(a)` FFT.
- `corr_pair(a,w)` as before.
- `exact_score(a)` fast.
- Normalization, resampling.
- Softmax LSE objective with corrected gradient:
```
def softmax_lse_grad(y, beta):
    n = len(y)+1
    z = np.empty(n); z[0]=0; z[1:]=y
    # clip to avoid overflow
    m = z.max(); if not finite set 0
    e = np.exp(z - m)
    a = e / e.sum()
    # compute d and weights
    c = fast_self_conv(a)
    max_c = c.max()
    d = float(n) * c  # S=1
    dmax = d.max()
    if dmax <=0: return 1e10, zeros
    zsoft = beta*(d-dmax)
    # prevent overflow: dmax; use stable
    e2 = np.exp(zsoft)
    sum_e = e2.sum()
    w = e2/sum_e
    L = dmax + np.log(sum_e)/beta
    LDS = np.dot(w,d)
    h = corr_pair(a,w)
    grad_a = 4.0*float(n)*h - 4.0*a*LDS
    # grad wrt y: same for j>0; verify ∑a grad=0 numerically maybe
    # project to remove any component along a? Since y0 fixed, gradient for y is grad_a (should be tangent). To be safe, subtract np.dot(a,grad_a)*a (small)
    dot = np.dot(a, grad_a)
    grad_y = grad_a - dot*a
    return 2.0*L, grad_y[:? length y] # y length n-1
```

Implementation details for FFT:
```
def next_power_of_two(m):
    m = int(max(1,m))
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```
`fast_self_conv(a)`:
- n = len(a); if n=0 empty; M=2*n-1; N=next_power_of_two(M)
- A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); out=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)[:M]
- Due floating error small negative; np.maximum? For score max, negative not issue; for d weights clip negative to 0? Convolution of nonnegative should be nonnegative; set out=np.nan_to_num; out=np.maximum(out,0).
Return out.

But for large a, if values ~1/n, c ~1/n; numerical OK. Use float64.

`corr_pair(a,w)`:
- w length 2n-1. M = n+len(w)-1 =3n-2? Wait w length 2n-1, a reversed length n -> conv length n+2n-1-1=3n-2. Need N next pow2(3n-2). Previous used M=n+int(w.size)-1 and returns [n-1:2n-1]. Check with N. Good.
```
N = next_power_of_two(n + len(w)-1)
A = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], N); W = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
full = np.fft.irfft(A*W, N)[:n+len(w)-1]
return full[n-1:2n-1]
```

def subgrad_polish(a, max_iters=20, deadline=None, rng=None):
   best=a; best_sc=exact_score(a)
   for it:
      c=fast_self_conv(a); cmax; if cmax<=0 break
      # active weights: top K powers
      best_improve=None
      for k in [1,2,3,5,8,16]:
         for eta in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8,1.5]:
            w = uniform top k c (or all within eps)
            h=corr_pair(a,w)
            # gradient sub for score 2n max c/S^2 with S=1: g =4n h -4 a*Cw? For exact max of d? Use ratio? Since a sum1. For single active: grad =4n h -4 a dmax. Normalize gradient by max(n?) We can compute g =4*(h - a*dot(w,c))? h=∑w a_{k-i}; Cw=dot(w,c). grad =4n(h? careful h dimension). Exact gradient for weighted d: g=4n h -4 a LDS with LDS=n Cw. normalize by gmax.
            g = 4.0*float(n)*h - 4.0*a*(float(n)*np.dot(w,c))
            gmax=abs(g).max()
            if gmax small break
            y? direct step: q = a - eta * g/gmax
            q = np.maximum(q,0); normalize
            sc=exact_score(q)
            if sc<best_sc: update
      if no improvement break
   return best
```

No lambdas/closures. All top-level.

`active_weights(c, k)`:
```
m=c.size
if k>=m: return ones/m
idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]  # careful if k=0
w=zeros; w[idx]=1/k; return w
```
`response_step(a, strength, k=5)`:
- c=fast_self_conv(a); S? normalize a sum1.
- w=active_weights(c,k)
- h=corr_pair(a,w); hm=max(abs(h)); if hm<=0 return copy
- q = a * np.exp(-strength * h/hm)
- normalize.
This moves mass away from high h (which contributes to active lags). Could also move to low h by `q = 1/(1 + strength*h/hm)`.

Subgrad polish:
```
def subgrad_polish(a, deadline, rng):
  best=normalize; best_sc=exact
  for it in range(40):
    if time>deadline break
    improved=False
    for k in (1,2,3,5,8,16):
      for strength in (0.05,0.15,0.3,0.6,1.2):
         if time>deadline break
         b=response_step(best, strength, k)
         for t in (0.2,0.5,0.8):
            q=normalize((1-t)*best + t*b)
            sc=exact(q)
            if sc<best_sc: update; best=q; improved=True
    if not improved: break
  return best_sc,best
```
This may be costly (many exact evals). Limit total few seconds by checking deadline. Since exact eval O(n log n), for n=10240 5s maybe 100 evals. We'll cap loop count.

In optimize_trajectory after each LSE stage, call `subgrad_polish` with small deadline (e.g., 5s). Or a separate final.

LSE direct run details:
```
def run_lbfgs_direct(a0, beta, deadline, maxfun):
    a = normalize_a(a0)
    n = len(a)
    best_a = a.copy(); best_sc = exact_score_a(a)
    # maybe if maxfun None estimate
    bounds = [(0.0, 10.0)]*n
    opts = {...}
    try:
       res = minimize(fun_lse_direct, a, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, args=(float(beta), PEN_SUM), bounds=bounds, options=opts)
    except Exception:
       return best_sc,best_a
    if res.x is not None:
       a2=normalize_a(res.x)
       sc=exact_score_a(a2)
       if sc<best_sc: return sc,a2
    return best_sc,best_a
```
Use `PEN_SUM=1e-3` as arg. For final high-beta maybe pen=1e-4? We can pass pen global.

In `fun_lse_direct`, if `a` sum S is far from1 due L-BFGS, d small. Could rescale inside for stability? If we compute p=a/S and L d(p) = n c(a)/S^2. Score=2L + pen(S-1)^2. Gradient formula correct. Good.

Let's re-verify gradient formula with d=n c/S^2 and L softmax. Score =2∑w d + pen. Weighted d: D=n Cw/S^2 (scalar). ∂/∂a_i of D = n/S^2 ∂c_i - 2n Cw a_i/S^3 = 2n h_i/S^2 - 2n Cw a_i/S^3. Multiply by 2 => 4n h_i/S^2 -4n Cw a_i/S^3. LDS=∑w d = n Cw/S^2. Second = -4 a_i (n Cw/S^2)/S = -4 a_i LDS/S. Yes.
Pen derivative =2pen(S-1). Good.

What about weights w derivative? L is smooth max, derivative wrt d is w, we accounted. Good.

`LDS` can be close to dmax. `h` from `corr_pair(a,w)`. For direct a not normalized, h scale. OK.

Need ensure `fast_self_conv` for a with zeros and small values. Use `np.maximum(out,0)`; but gradient h using a and w, if c negative due roundoff, dmax maybe 0? For nonnegative, no issue.

Could we skip `fast_self_conv` in fun when beta high and use exact max? No.

Time allocation function `optimize_trajectory`:
```
def optimize_trajectory(a0, deadline, label=''):
    a = normalize_a(a0)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score_a(a)
    n=len(a)
    start=time.monotonic()
    total=max(10.0, deadline-start)
    print start
    stages = [(20,0.04),(60,0.07),(200,0.10),(600,0.12),(2000,0.15),(6000,0.18),(20000,0.17),(60000,0.17)] # sum 1
    cum=0
    for beta,frac in stages:
       cum += frac
       if cum>1: cum=1
       sub_dead = start + cum*total
       sub_dead = min(sub_dead, deadline-5)
       while time < sub_dead - 5:
          rem = sub_dead - now
          if rem < 5: break
          mf = estimate_maxfun(rem*0.9,n)
          if mf<64: break
          sc,a2=run_lbfgs_direct(a,beta,deadline,mf)
          if sc<best_sc: update print
          a=normalize_a(a2)
       if time < sub_dead-5 and stage not last:
          # subgrad quick
          sc_sg,a_sg=subgrad_polish(a, sub_dead-5, max_evals=20)
          if sc_sg<best_sc: update
          a=normalize_a(a_sg)
    return best_sc,best_a
```
But repeat L-BFGS in while same beta may just continue from current; could call multiple times until sub_dead. Good.

`exact_score_a(a)`:
```
def exact_score_a(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0, posinf=1e6, neginf=0)
    a=np.clip(a,0,None)
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S<1e-12: return np.inf
    c=fast_self_conv(a)
    cmax=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if cmax<0: cmax=0
    return float(2.0*a.size*cmax/(S*S))
```
For score, using original n. If a normalized sum1. Good.

`fast_self_conv` maybe returns ndarray length 2n-1; if n large and a sum1, values small. Use `A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)`, `out = np.fft.irfft(A*A, N)[:M]`. Due small negative, `out = np.where(out>0,out,0)`? Use `np.maximum(out, 0.0)` for max. But for gradient h no c. OK.

`corr_pair`: w length may not be 2n-1? In LSE w length c length. Full length n+wlen-1. Return length n. Use `np.nan_to_num`.

```
def subgrad_polish(a, deadline, max_steps=30, rng=None):
    best=normalize_a(a); best_sc=exact_score_a(best)
    n=len(best)
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(12345)
    ks=(1,2,3,5,8)
    sts=(0.05,0.15,0.35,0.75)
    ts=(0.15,0.4,0.7)
    for it in range(max_steps):
       if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
       improved=False
       for k in ks:
         if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
         b=response_step(best, 0.5, k)  # maybe generate once per k? but strength varies.
         for st in sts:
            b=response_step(best, st, k)
            for t in ts:
               if time... break
               q=normalize_a((1.0-t)*best + t*b)
               sc=exact_score_a(q)
               if sc<best_sc:
                   best_sc=sc; best=q.copy(); improved=True
       if not improved: break
    return best_sc,best
```
This can be too many: per step 5*4*3=60 exact evals, for n=10240 2s. max_steps 30 too much. We'll limit via deadline: pass small deadline. In optimize, call after stages with deadline=now+5. In final, 20s. Could still too many but ok. Maybe reduce loops: ks (1,3,8), sts (0.2,0.6,1.2), ts (0.3,0.6) =>18/step. Good.

Implement `response_step` calls `fast_self_conv` and `corr_pair`; expensive. Each response includes exact eval for b/t? The `b` not scored unless t=1 maybe; our q eval does exact. For each st/k we compute b. That's 3*3=9 FFT pairs; each q exact 3 evals -> 27 exact. Could be heavy. We can compute b once per k with default strength? Maybe use gradient sub with a few eta. Simpler: use direct subgrad gradient step and one eval per eta:
```
def subgrad_step(a, strength, k):
   c=fast_self_conv(a); w=active_weights(c,k); h=corr_pair(a,w)
   g = 4*n*h - 4*a*n*dot(w,c)
   gmax=abs(g).max()
   q = a - strength * g/gmax
   q=np.maximum(q,0); normalize; return q
```
Then exact eval q. This is cheaper: one corr per step. We'll have fewer.
```

      q=subgrad_step(best, st, k)
      sc=exact(q); if improved...
```

`normalize_a`:
```
def normalize_a(a, target=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0: return np.array([1.0])
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1e6, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-15:
       return np.full(a.size, target/a.size)
    return (target/s)*a
```
If n large and target1/n small, OK.

`exact_score_a`: use a maybe non-normalized. If n==1? conv[0]=a0^2, score=2*a0^2/a0^2=2. Good.

`resample_sequence`: if a size 1 and n>1, return full? `np.interp` works if x_old one? It returns fill? Actually np.interp with xp length1 works? It returns constant? Maybe. Handle m==1: return full(n, a[0]/n? normalize later) if a[0]>0. But if m=1 and sum? We'll do if m==1: return np.full(n, float(a[0]) / n) (sum same). For m>1, interp.

`generate_inits` implementation details:
```
def generate_inits(n, rng, parent=None, cap=24):
    inits=[]
    if parent is not None and parent.size>0:
       append_init(inits, resample_sequence(parent,n), cap)
       append_init(inits, resample_sequence(parent[::-1],n), cap)
    x = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    append... ones
    for rate in (1,2,4,8,12): exp(-rate*x), exp(-rate*(1-x)), sum? But sum exp can be high; normalize.
    for p in (0.5,0.7,1,1.5,2,3): x**p, (1-x)**p
    for alpha in (0.3,0.7,1.0,1.5): 1+alpha*x, 1-alpha*x (clip positive)
    for f in (1,2,3,4): 1+0.7*np.sin(2πf x + phase), 1-0.7...; phases fixed 0,0.7,1.5
    # random low-pass:
    for _ in range(4):
       Nf=next_power_of_two(n)
       freqs=np.fft.rfftfreq(Nf)*Nf
       pwr=1/((np.abs(freqs)+1)**rng.uniform(1.5,4.0)); pwr[0]=0
       spec=pwr*np.exp(1j*rng.uniform(-np.pi,np.pi,len(pwr)))
       noise=np.fft.irfft(spec,Nf)[:n]
       noise=noise-noise.mean()
       vals=1.0 + float(rng.uniform(0.3,1.0))*noise
       append(vals)
    # random blocks
    for nb in (8,16,32): if nb < n: vals = np.repeat(0.1+0.9*rng.random(nb), n//nb); adjust length; append
    # binary support
    for cfac in (1.8,2.3,2.8):
       m=int(cf*np.sqrt(n)); m=min(max(4,m),n)
       vals=np.full(n,1e-3)
       if m<n: idx=np.sort(rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)); vals[idx]=1.0
       append
    return inits
```
Need avoid creating arrays for n huge many times? OK.
For n=32768, Fourier random 4 times uses Nf 32768 FFT, small. Good.

Now code details for `optimize_trajectory` with non-full stages:
```
def optimize_trajectory(a0, deadline, label='', full=True):
    a=normalize_a(a0)
    n=len(a)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score_a(a)
    start=time.monotonic()
    total=max(20.0, deadline-start)
    if full:
       stages=[(20,0.04),(60,0.07),(200,0.10),(600,0.12),(2000,0.15),(6000,0.18),(20000,0.18),(60000,0.16)]
    else:
       stages=[(200,0.15),(1000,0.20),(4000,0.25),(15000,0.25),(50000,0.15)]
    cum=0
    for beta, frac in stages:
       cum += frac
       if cum>1: cum=1
       sub_dead = start + cum*total
       sub_dead = min(sub_dead, deadline-5)
       while time.monotonic() < sub_dead - 5:
          rem = sub_dead - time.monotonic()
          if rem < 5: break
          mf=estimate_maxfun(rem*0.9, n)
          if mf<64: break
          sc,a2=run_lbfgs_direct(a, float(beta), deadline, mf)
          if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
             best_sc=sc; best_a=a2.copy(); print
          a=normalize_a(a2)
       # quick subgrad if time before sub_dead and not too small
       sg_dead = min(sub_dead - 5, time.monotonic()+max(5, 0.02*total))
       if sg_dead > time.monotonic()+3:
          sc_sg,a_sg=subgrad_polish(a, sg_dead, rng=None)
          ...
       if best score not improved a lot? continue
    return best_sc,best_a
```
Need pass rng? subgrad_polish can use deterministic; no rng needed.

def estimate_maxfun(rem, n):
    rem=max(1.0, float(rem))
    est=estimate_eval_time(n)
    mf=int((rem*0.7)/max(est,1e-4))
    return max(64, min(2500, mf))
```

Let's define `subgrad_step(a, strength, k)` exactly:
```
def subgrad_step(a, strength, k):
    a=normalize_a(a)
    n=len(a)
    c=fast_self_conv(a)
    cmax=c.max()
    if cmax <=0: return a
    w=active_weights(c,k)
    Cw=float(np.dot(w,c))
    h=corr_pair(a,w)
    g = 4.0*float(n)*h - 4.0*a*(float(n)*Cw)
    gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
    if gmax < 1e-14: return a
    q = a - strength * (g/gmax)
    q = np.maximum(q,0.0)
    return normalize_a(q)
```
Note if strength too large, q negative; clip. This is step in a-space, not log. It can set zeros. Fine. Since a scale ~1/n, gmax ~O(1)? step values: q diff ~ strength*O(1) >>1/n if g normalized by gmax, too large? Let's estimate at S=1, gmax maybe 4 for active endpoint? a_i ~1/n=1e-5. gmax O(1). Dividing by gmax gives O(1), step strength 0.01 gives absolute 0.01, much larger than a_i, q clips to zero many. That's not good. We should normalize g relative to a scale. Use `g_scaled = g / (a.mean()*n)`? Actually gradient of score wrt a is O(1); moving in a requires step O(1/n) to change relative values. In L-BFGS with direct a, variables scale ~1/n; line search step sizes adjust. For subgrad, we should step in log space or normalize gradient by `a.sum()*?`. A better: Use multiplicative response based on `h/hmax` which is scale ~1 (or 0.1). `q = a * exp(-strength * h/hmax)`. This changes relative not absolute. Let's use that for subgrad.
- `hmax = max(h)`; if hmax small. `q=a * np.exp(-strength * h/hmax)`. Since h_i maybe 0 at some, positive. This is previous. It preserves positivity. Strength 0.1-1. Good. We'll implement `subgrad_step` as multiplicative response with active weights top k.
Could also use `1/(1+strength*h/hmax)`. Exponential ok.
```
def active_response(a, strength, k):
   c=fast_self_conv(a)
   w=active_weights(c,k)
   h=corr_pair(a,w)
   hm=max(h)
   if hm<=0: return a
   q = a * np.exp(-strength * h/hm)
   return normalize_a(q)
```
This moves mass away from positions contributing to active lags. To improve, mass should go to low h positions; exponential does. Strength moderate 0.1-1. Good.

def make_lp_rows(a, k_idx):
    n=len(a); rows=[]
    for k in k_idx:
       row=np.zeros(n)
       lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,k)
       # j in lo..hi, coeff a[k-j]
       # j=lo -> a[k-lo], j=hi -> a[k-hi]
       seg = a[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1]? Let's test: j=hi, index k-hi; j=lo, index k-lo. We need array indices increasing j: [a[k-lo], a[k-lo-1],...,a[k-hi]]. That's a[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1].
       row[lo:hi+1]=a[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1]
       rows.append(row)
    return np.vstack(rows)
```

def cross_conv(a,b):
  n=len(a); m=len(b); M2=n+m-1; N=next_power_of_two(M2)
  return np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a,N)*np.fft.rfft(b,N),N)[:M2]
```

Detailed functions:

```
def make_lp_rows(a, k_idx):
    n=len(a)
    rows=[]
    for k in k_idx:
        row=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        lo = max(0, k-(n-1))
        hi = min(n-1, k)
        if hi >= lo:
            row[lo:hi+1] = a[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1]
        rows.append(row)
    if not rows:
        return np.zeros((0,n), dtype=np.float64)
    return np.vstack(rows)
```
Check slice when k-hi negative? lo ensures k-hi>=0. k-lo+1 <=? hi≤k => k-hi≥0, k-lo≤k-lo; if k<2n-2 then k-lo < n? Since lo≥k-n+1, k-lo ≤ n-1. OK.

`cross_conv` for LP violation:
```
def cross_conv(a,b):
   n=len(a); m=len(b); M=len(a)+len(b)-1; N=next_power_of_two(M)
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); B=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
   out=np.fft.irfft(A*B,N)[:M]
   return np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
```
For same orientation as np.convolve: yes.

`lp_response`:
```
def lp_response(a, max_iter=4, max_rows=80, time_limit=8.0):
    a=normalize_a(a)
    n=len(a)
    if n < 4 or n > 65536: return a.copy()
    c=fast_self_conv(a); M=float(np.max(c))
    if M<=1e-15: return a.copy()
    a_lp=normalize_a(np.maximum(a, 1e-12))
    m=2*n-1
    initial_k=16
    idx=np.argpartition(c, -initial_k)[-initial_k:]
    in_rows=np.zeros(m, dtype=bool); in_rows[idx]=True
    b=a.copy()
    c_obj=-np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64)
    bounds=[(0.0, 1e8)]*n
    add_count=8
    for it in range(max_iter):
       rows=k_idx list (idx)
       if len(rows) > max_rows: rows = rows[:max_rows]? But k_idx unique; cap.
       A=make_lp_rows(a_lp, rows)
       rhs=M*np.ones(len(rows))
       try:
          res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=rhs, bounds=bounds, method='highs',
                      options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': float(time_limit)})
       except Exception:
          break
       if res is not None and getattr(res,'x',None) is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
          b=np.maximum(res.x,0.0)
          S=float(b.sum())
          if S>1e-12:
             b = b/S
          else:
             break
          c_ab=cross_conv(a,b)
          # violation
          thr = M*(1.0+1e-8) + 1e-15
          viol_idx=np.where(c_ab > thr)[0]
          if viol_idx.size == 0:
             break
          unm = viol_idx[~in_rows[viol_idx]]
          if unm.size == 0:
             break
          if len(rows) + min(add_count, unm.size) > max_rows:
             unm = unm[:max(1, max_rows-len(rows))]
          rows = np.concatenate([rows, unm])
          in_rows[unm]=True

def lp_polish(a, deadline, max_rounds=3):
    best=normalize_a(a); best_sc=exact_score_a(best)
    n=len(best)

    time_limit = 5.0 if n<=16384 else 10.0

       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       b=lp_response(best, max_iter=3, max_rows=50 if n>16384 else 80, time_limit=time_limit)
       if b is None or not finite: break
       improved=False
       for t in ts:
          if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
          q=normalize_a((1.0-float(t))*best + float(t)*b)
          sc=exact_score_a(q)
          if sc<best_sc:
              best_sc=sc; best=q.copy(); improved=True
       if not improved: break
    return best_sc,best
```

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative sequence minimizing
    2 * n * max(conv(a, a)) / sum(a)^2.

The program uses several complementary ideas:

1. FFT-based exact scoring and FFT-based gradients for a smooth
   log-sum-exp approximation of the supremum.
2. Standard L-BFGS-B local optimization with a continuation schedule.
3. Active-set / Frank-Wolfe style moves that reduce current peak
   coefficients by shifting mass away from high-correlation positions.
4. A constraint-generation linear program for the "best response"
   sequence b given the current sequence a:
       max sum b  s.t.  conv(a,b) <= max(conv(a,a)), b >= 0.
   This is used sparingly as a powerful escape/local-search tool.
5. Coarse-to-fine / resampling across a few lengths, capped at a length
   that is still comfortable for the evaluator.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import minimize

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None

PEN_SUM = 1e-2
MAX_OPT_N = 16384


def next_power_of_two(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def fast_self_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    M = 2 * n - 1
    N = next_power_of_two(M)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    out = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)[:M]
    out = np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return np.maximum(out, 0.0)


def cross_conv(a, b):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    m = int(b.size)
    if n == 0 or m == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    M = n + m - 1
    N = next_power_of_two(M)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    B = np.fft.rfft(b, N)
    out = np.fft.irfft(A * B, N)[:M]
    out = np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return np.maximum(out, 0.0)


def corr_pair(a, w):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0 or w.size == 0:
        return np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    M = n + int(w.size) - 1
    N = next_power_of_two(M)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], N)
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    full = np.fft.irfft(A * W, N)[:M]
    return full[n - 1:2 * n - 1]


def normalize_a(a, target=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1e6, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(a.size, float(target) / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return (float(target) / s) * a


def exact_score_a(a):
    a = normalize_a(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    c = fast_self_conv(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if cmax < 0.0:
        cmax = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax / (S * S))


def resample_sequence(a, n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    m = int(a.size)
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if m == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    if m == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(a[0]) / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a).astype(np.float64)
    return out


def active_weights(c, k):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = int(c.size)
    if m <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    k = int(max(1, min(k, m)))
    if k >= m:
        return np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
    idx = np.argpartition(c, m - k)[m - k:]
    w = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    w[idx] = 1.0 / float(k)
    return w


def active_response(a, strength, k):
    a = normalize_a(a)
    c = fast_self_conv(a)
    w = active_weights(c, k)
    h = corr_pair(a, w)
    hm = float(np.max(np.abs(h))) if h.size else 0.0
    if hm <= 1e-15:
        return a
    q = a * np.exp(-float(strength) * (h / hm))
    return normalize_a(q)


def subgrad_polish(a, deadline, max_rounds=5):
    best = normalize_a(a)
    best_sc = exact_score_a(best)
    n = int(best.size)
    if n < 4:
        return best_sc, best

    ks = (1, 4, 8, 16)
    strengths = (0.35, 1.1)
    ts = (0.25, 0.55)

    for _ in range(int(max_rounds)):
        if time.monotonic() >= float(deadline):
            break
        improved = False
        for k in ks:
            if time.monotonic() >= float(deadline):
                break
            for strength in strengths:
                if time.monotonic() >= float(deadline):
                    break
                b = active_response(best, strength, k)
                for t in ts:
                    if time.monotonic() >= float(deadline):
                        break
                    tt = float(t)
                    q = normalize_a((1.0 - tt) * best + tt * b)
                    sc = exact_score_a(q)
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                        best_sc = sc
                        best = q.copy()
                        improved = True
        if not improved:
            break
    return best_sc, best


def estimate_eval_time(n):
    n = float(max(16, n))
    base = 0.025
    scale = max(0.5, n / 8192.0)
    log_scale = max(1.0, float(np.log2(max(16.0, n))) / 13.0)
    return base * scale * log_scale


def estimate_maxfun(remaining, n):
    rem = max(1.0, float(remaining))
    if rem < 4.0:
        return 64
    est = estimate_eval_time(n)
    mf = int((rem * 0.72) / max(est, 1e-4))
    return max(64, min(2500, mf))


def lse_score_grad(a, beta, pen):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1e6, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    n = int(a.size)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-8:
        return 1e10, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    beta = float(beta)
    if not np.isfinite(beta) or beta <= 1e-6:
        beta = 50.0
    if beta > 5e6:
        beta = 5e6

    c = fast_self_conv(a)
    d = (float(n) * c) / (S * S)
    dmax = float(np.max(d)) if d.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(dmax) or dmax <= 1e-15:
        return 1e10, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    z = beta * (d - dmax)
    e = np.exp(z)
    sum_e = float(np.sum(e))
    if sum_e <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(sum_e):
        w = active_weights(d, 1)
        L = dmax
    else:
        w = e / sum_e
        L = dmax + np.log(sum_e) / beta

    LDS = float(np.dot(w, d))
    h = corr_pair(a, w)

    # Derivative of 2 * sum_k w_k * n*conv(a,a)_k / sum(a)^2.
    # The denominator derivative contributes a constant per coefficient.
    grad = (4.0 * float(n) * h) / (S * S)
    grad -= (4.0 * LDS) / S
    grad += 2.0 * float(pen) * (S - 1.0)

    grad = np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    grad = np.clip(grad, -1e6, 1e6)
    val = float(2.0 * L + float(pen) * (S - 1.0) * (S - 1.0))
    return val, grad


def run_lbfgs_direct(a0, beta, deadline, maxfun=None):
    a = normalize_a(a0)
    n = int(a.size)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = exact_score_a(a)

    rem = float(deadline - time.monotonic())
    if rem < 5.0 or n < 2:
        return best_sc, best_a

    if maxfun is None:
        maxfun = estimate_maxfun(rem * 0.9, n)
    maxfun = int(max(maxfun, 64))
    maxfun = int(min(maxfun, 2500))

    est = estimate_eval_time(n)
    if rem < (6.0 * est + 10.0):
        return best_sc, best_a

    bounds = [(0.0, 10.0)] * n
    opts = {
        "m": 20,
        "ftol": 1e-14,
        "gtol": 1e-12,
        "maxls": 50,
        "maxiter": max(30, maxfun // 2),
        "maxfun": maxfun,
    }

    try:
        res = minimize(
            lse_score_grad,
            a,
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            jac=True,
            args=(float(beta), float(PEN_SUM)),
            bounds=bounds,
            options=opts,
        )
    except Exception:
        return best_sc, best_a

    x = getattr(res, "x", None)
    if x is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        a2 = normalize_a(x)
        sc2 = exact_score_a(a2)
        if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc2
            best_a = a2.copy()
    return best_sc, best_a


def make_lp_rows(a, k_idx):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    rows = []
    for k in k_idx:
        k = int(k)
        row = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        lo = max(0, k - (n - 1))
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if hi >= lo:
            row[lo:hi + 1] = a[k - hi:k - lo + 1][::-1]
        rows.append(row)
    if not rows:
        return np.zeros((0, n), dtype=np.float64)
    return np.vstack(rows)


def lp_response(a, max_iter=3, max_rows=80, time_limit=8.0):
    a = normalize_a(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if linprog is None or n < 4 or n > MAX_OPT_N:
        return a.copy()

    c = fast_self_conv(a)
    M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if M <= 1e-15:
        return a.copy()

    # A tiny floor avoids unbounded variables caused by exact zeros.
    if float(np.min(a)) > 1e-10:
        a_lp = a.copy()
        M_lp = M
    else:
        a_lp = normalize_a(np.maximum(a, 1e-12))
        c_lp = fast_self_conv(a_lp)
        M_lp = float(np.max(c_lp)) if c_lp.size else 0.0
    M = max(M, M_lp, 1e-15)

    m_total = 2 * n - 1
    initial_k = min(16, m_total)
    if initial_k <= 0:
        return a.copy()
    if initial_k < m_total:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, m_total - initial_k)[m_total - initial_k:]
    else:
        idx = np.arange(m_total)

    in_rows = np.zeros(m_total, dtype=bool)
    in_rows[idx] = True
    rows = idx.astype(int, copy=True)

    c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1e8)] * n
    b = a.copy()
    add_count = 8

    for _ in range(int(max_iter)):
        if rows.size == 0:
            break
        if rows.size > max_rows:
            rows = rows[:max_rows]
            in_rows = np.zeros(m_total, dtype=bool)
            in_rows[rows] = True

        A = make_lp_rows(a_lp, rows)
        if A.size == 0:
            break
        A = A / M
        rhs = np.ones(rows.size, dtype=np.float64)

        try:
            options = {
                "presolve": True,
                "time_limit": float(time_limit),
                "max_iter": 20000,
            }
            res = linprog(
                c_obj,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=rhs,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs",
                options=options,
            )
        except Exception:
            break

        x = getattr(res, "x", None)
        if x is None or not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
            break

        b = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        b = np.nan_to_num(b, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
        S = float(b.sum())
        if S <= 1e-12:
            break
        b = b / S

        c_ab = cross_conv(a, b)
        thr = M * (1.0 + 1e-8) + 1e-15
        viol = np.where(c_ab > thr)[0]
        if viol.size == 0:
            break

        new = viol[~in_rows[viol]]
        if new.size == 0:
            break

        room = max_rows - int(rows.size)
        if room <= 0:
            break
        new = new[:min(add_count, room)]
        rows = np.concatenate([rows, new])
        in_rows[new] = True

    return normalize_a(b)


def lp_polish(a, deadline, max_rounds=2):
    best = normalize_a(a)
    best_sc = exact_score_a(best)
    n = int(best.size)
    if n < 8 or n > MAX_OPT_N:
        return best_sc, best

    time_limit = 5.0 if n <= 10240 else 8.0
    max_rows = 70 if n <= 10240 else 50
    ts = (0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.50)

    for _ in range(int(max_rounds)):
        if time.monotonic() >= float(deadline):
            break
        b = lp_response(best, max_iter=3, max_rows=max_rows, time_limit=time_limit)
        if b is None or not np.all(np.isfinite(b)):
            break
        b = normalize_a(b)

        improved = False
        for t in ts:
            if time.monotonic() >= float(deadline):
                break
            tt = float(t)
            q = normalize_a((1.0 - tt) * best + tt * b)
            sc = exact_score_a(q)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best = q.copy()
                improved = True
        if not improved:
            break

    return best_sc, best


def append_init(lst, arr, cap):
    if len(lst) >= int(cap):
        return
    a = normalize_a(arr)
    if a.size > 0:
        lst.append(a)


def generate_inits(n, rng, parent=None, cap=20):
    inits = []
    n = int(max(4, n))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(rng) if not hasattr(rng, "integers") else rng)

    if parent is not None and parent.size > 0:
        append_init(inits, resample_sequence(parent, n), cap)
        append_init(inits, resample_sequence(parent[::-1], n), cap)

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)

    append_init(inits, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), cap)

    for rate in (1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 8.0, 12.0):
        append_init(inits, np.exp(-rate * x), cap)
        append_init(inits, np.exp(-rate * (1.0 - x)), cap)
        append_init(inits, np.exp(-rate * x) + np.exp(-rate * (1.0 - x)), cap)

    for p in (0.5, 0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0):
        append_init(inits, np.power(x, p), cap)
        append_init(inits, np.power(1.0 - x, p), cap)
        append_init(inits, np.power(x, p) + np.power(1.0 - x, p), cap)

    for alpha in (0.3, 0.7, 1.0, 1.5):
        append_init(inits, 1.0 + alpha * x, cap)
        append_init(inits, 1.0 - alpha * x, cap)
        append_init(inits, 1.0 + alpha * np.abs(2.0 * x - 1.0), cap)

    for f in (1, 2, 3, 4):
        for ph in (0.0, 0.7, 1.5, np.pi):
            append_init(inits, 1.0 + 0.7 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * f * x + ph), cap)
            append_init(inits, 1.0 + 0.7 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f * x + ph), cap)

    Nf = next_power_of_two(n)
    for _ in range(4):
        freqs = np.fft.rfftfreq(Nf) * Nf
        power = 1.0 / ((np.abs(freqs) + 1.0) ** float(rng.uniform(1.5, 4.0)))
        power[0] = 0.0
        phase = rng.uniform(-np.pi, np.pi, size=len(power))
        spec = power * np.exp(1j * phase)
        spec[0] = 0.0
        noise = np.fft.irfft(spec, Nf)[:n]
        noise = noise - float(noise.mean())
        vals = 1.0 + float(rng.uniform(0.3, 1.0)) * noise
        append_init(inits, vals, cap)

    for nb in (8, 16, 32):
        if nb >= n:
            nb = max(2, n // 2)
        vals = 0.1 + 0.9 * rng.random(nb)
        block = np.repeat(vals, max(1, n // nb))
        if block.size < n:
            block = np.concatenate([block, np.repeat(vals[-1], n - block.size)])
        append_init(inits, block[:n], cap)

    # Binary / sparse-style starting points. These can lead to different basins.
    for cf in (1.6, 2.2, 2.8):
        m = int(cf * float(np.sqrt(n)))
        m = int(max(4, min(n, m)))
        vals = np.full(n, 1e-3, dtype=np.float64)
        if m < n:
            idx = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)
            vals[idx] = 1.0
        append_init(inits, vals, cap)

    for _ in range(2):
        vals = 0.05 + 0.95 * rng.random(n)
        vals = vals * np.exp(0.4 * rng.standard_normal(n))
        append_init(inits, vals, cap)

    return inits


def score_key(item):
    return item[0]


def prepare_starts(lengths, parent, rng, cap=18):
    scored = []
    seen = set()
    for raw_n in lengths:
        n = int(raw_n)
        if n < 256:
            n = 256
        if n > MAX_OPT_N:
            n = MAX_OPT_N
        if n in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(n)
        arrs = generate_inits(n, rng, parent, cap=cap)
        for arr in arrs:
            sc = exact_score_a(arr)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                scored.append((sc, arr))
    scored.sort(key=score_key)
    return scored


def optimize_trajectory(a0, deadline, label="", full=True):
    a = normalize_a(a0)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = exact_score_a(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    start = time.monotonic()
    total = max(20.0, float(deadline) - start)

    print("Optimize [{}] n={} initial_score={:.8f} budget={:.1f}s".format(
        label, n, best_sc, total))

    if full:
        stages = [
            (20.0, 0.04),
            (60.0, 0.07),
            (200.0, 0.10),
            (600.0, 0.12),
            (2000.0, 0.15),
            (6000.0, 0.18),
            (20000.0, 0.18),
            (60000.0, 0.16),
        ]
    else:
        stages = [
            (200.0, 0.15),
            (1000.0, 0.20),
            (4000.0, 0.25),
            (15000.0, 0.25),
            (50000.0, 0.15),
        ]

    cum = 0.0
    for beta, frac in stages:
        cum += float(frac)
        if cum > 1.0:
            cum = 1.0
        sub_dead = start + cum * total
        sub_dead = min(float(sub_dead), float(deadline) - 5.0)

        while time.monotonic() < sub_dead - 5.0:
            rem = float(sub_dead - time.monotonic())
            if rem < 10.0:
                break
            mf = estimate_maxfun(rem * 0.9, n)
            if mf < 64:
                break
            before = best_sc
            sc, a2 = run_lbfgs_direct(a, float(beta), deadline, mf)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best_a = a2.copy()
                print("  [{}] improvement {:.8f} beta={:.0f} elapsed={:.1f}s".format(
                    label, best_sc, beta, time.monotonic() - start))
            a = normalize_a(a2)
            if not (sc < before):
                break

        # Active set / LP polish at selected continuation points.
        now = time.monotonic()
        if n <= MAX_OPT_N and beta in (2000.0, 60000.0) and now < sub_dead - 10.0:
            lp_dead = min(float(sub_dead) - 5.0, now + 35.0)
            if lp_dead > now + 8.0 and linprog is not None:
                sc_lp, a_lp = lp_polish(a, lp_dead, max_rounds=2)
                if np.isfinite(sc_lp) and sc_lp < best_sc:
                    best_sc = sc_lp
                    best_a = a_lp.copy()
                    print("  [{}] LP polish {:.8f} after beta={:.0f}".format(
                        label, best_sc, beta))
                a = normalize_a(a_lp)

        if beta == 60000.0 or not full:
            if now < sub_dead - 8.0:
                sg_dead = min(float(sub_dead) - 4.0, now + 20.0)
                if sg_dead > now + 3.0:
                    sc_sg, a_sg = subgrad_polish(a, sg_dead, max_rounds=4)
                    if np.isfinite(sc_sg) and sc_sg < best_sc:
                        best_sc = sc_sg
                        best_a = a_sg.copy()
                        print("  [{}] subgrad polish {:.8f} after beta={:.0f}".format(
                            label, best_sc, beta))
                    a = normalize_a(a_sg)

    print("End [{}] best={:.8f} n={}".format(label, best_sc, n))
    return best_sc, best_a


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(300.0, float(budget_s))
    reserve = min(180.0, max(90.0, 0.05 * total_budget))
    deadline = t0 + total_budget - reserve
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    parent = None
    try:
        h = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if h is not None:
            p = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1e6, neginf=0.0)
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if p.size >= 128:
                parent = p
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None:
        primary_len = int(parent.size)
    else:
        primary_len = 10240
    primary_len = int(max(256, min(MAX_OPT_N, primary_len)))

    print("Using length cap {}. Parent present: {} length {}".format(
        MAX_OPT_N, parent is not None, int(parent.size) if parent is not None else 0))

    cap_primary = 22 if primary_len <= 10240 else 16
    scored = prepare_starts([primary_len], parent, rng, cap=cap_primary)

    if parent is not None:
        primary_start = normalize_a(resample_sequence(parent, primary_len))
        primary_sc = exact_score_a(primary_start)
    else:
        if scored:
            primary_start = normalize_a(scored[0][1])
            primary_sc = scored[0][0]
        else:
            primary_start = np.ones(primary_len, dtype=np.float64) / float(primary_len)
            primary_sc = exact_score_a(primary_start)

    if scored and scored[0][0] < primary_sc:
        primary_start = normalize_a(scored[0][1])
        primary_sc = scored[0][0]
        print("Using best generated primary init, score={:.8f}".format(primary_sc))
    else:
        print("Using parent/primary start, score={:.8f}".format(primary_sc))

    best_seq = primary_start.copy()
    best_score = primary_sc

    work = max(120.0, total_budget - 180.0)
    primary_dead = t0 + 0.55 * work
    primary_dead = min(primary_dead, deadline - 120.0)

    sc, seq = optimize_trajectory(best_seq, primary_dead, "primary", full=True)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        best_score = sc
        best_seq = seq.copy()

    # Secondary length: one finer resample, if it makes sense.
    secondary_len = min(MAX_OPT_N, 2 * int(best_seq.size))
    secondary_dead = t0 + 0.85 * work
    secondary_dead = min(secondary_dead, deadline - 120.0)

    if secondary_len != int(best_seq.size) and time.monotonic() < secondary_dead - 120.0:
        starts = []
        append_init(starts, resample_sequence(best_seq, secondary_len), cap=4)
        if parent is not None:
            append_init(starts, resample_sequence(parent, secondary_len), cap=4)
        # A generated smooth at the secondary length too.
        cap2 = 10 if secondary_len <= 12000 else 8
        gen2 = generate_inits(secondary_len, rng, parent, cap=cap2)
        scored2 = []
        for arr in gen2:
            scx = exact_score_a(arr)
            if np.isfinite(scx):
                scored2.append((scx, arr))
        scored2.sort(key=score_key)
        for _, arr in scored2[:2]:
            append_init(starts, arr, cap=6)

        sec_start = starts[0] if starts else resample_sequence(best_seq, secondary_len)
        sec_sc = exact_score_a(sec_start)
        for arr in starts[1:]:
            scx = exact_score_a(arr)
            if np.isfinite(scx) and scx < sec_sc:
                sec_sc = scx
                sec_start = normalize_a(arr)

        print("Secondary length={}, start_score={:.8f}".format(secondary_len, sec_sc))
        sc, seq = optimize_trajectory(sec_start, secondary_dead, "secondary", full=False)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = seq.copy()
            print("Secondary improved to {:.8f}".format(best_score))

    # Final polish on the current best.
    now = time.monotonic()
    if now < deadline - 25.0:
        sg_dead = min(deadline - 10.0, now + max(20.0, 0.05 * total_budget))
        sc, seq = subgrad_polish(best_seq, sg_dead, max_rounds=6)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = seq.copy()

    now = time.monotonic()
    if now < deadline - 15.0:
        mf = estimate_maxfun((deadline - 5.0) - now, int(best_seq.size))
        if mf >= 64:
            sc, seq = run_lbfgs_direct(best_seq, 20000.0, deadline - 5.0, mf)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_seq = seq.copy()

    now = time.monotonic()
    if now < deadline - 10.0 and linprog is not None and int(best_seq.size) <= MAX_OPT_N:
        lp_dead = deadline - 3.0
        lp_dead = min(lp_dead, now + max(25.0, 0.04 * total_budget))
        if lp_dead > now + 8.0:
            sc, seq = lp_polish(best_seq, lp_dead, max_rounds=1)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_seq = seq.copy()

    out_arr = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    out_arr = np.nan_to_num(out_arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    out_arr = np.clip(out_arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(out_arr.sum())
    if out_arr.size == 0 or S < 0.01:
        out_arr = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) * 0.5
        S = float(out_arr.sum())
    out_arr = out_arr / S
    out_arr = np.clip(out_arr, 0.0, 1000.0)

    out = [float(x) for x in out_arr]

    print("FINAL internal_score={:.8f} length={} elapsed={:.1f}s".format(
        best_score, len(out), time.monotonic() - t0))

    # Avoid spending evaluation time near the hard limit.  Internal exact score
    # uses the same arithmetic as evaluate_sequence for valid nonnegative inputs.
    return out
```
